# agent_3: the whole run

[user message]
For a positive integer \(s\), consider the following iteration in exact real arithmetic. Let \(n\ge1\), let \(A\in\mathbb R^{n\times n}\) satisfy \(A^\top=A\) and \(v^\top Av>0\) for every nonzero \(v\in\mathbb R^n\), and choose \(b,x_0\in\mathbb R^n\). Define
\[
x_*=A^{-1}b,\qquad r_k=b-Ax_k,\qquad \|v\|_A=(v^\top Av)^{1/2}.
\]
If \(r_k=0\), terminate. Otherwise, set
\[
\mathcal K_s(A,r_k)=\operatorname{span}\{r_k,Ar_k,\ldots,A^{s-1}r_k\}
\]
and let \(x_{k+1}\) be the unique minimizer of \(\|x_*-z\|_A\) over \(z\in x_k+\mathcal K_s(A,r_k)\). Each such minimization is one restart block, so \(k\) counts blocks. For a nonterminating orbit, define
\[
y_k=\frac{r_k}{\|r_k\|_2},\qquad \|v\|_2=(v^\top v)^{1/2}.
\]
Determine the smallest positive integer \(s\) for which there exist \(n,A,b,x_0\) as above such that \(r_k\ne0\) for every \(k\ge0\), but \((y_{2k})_{k\ge0}\) does not converge in Euclidean norm. Return \(+\infty\) if no such integer exists.

The answer is 4. This was proved recently, so it is not an open problem: your team's task is to prove it, completely, and to write Python that really computes or checks every step that can be checked. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at any other answer is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving 4 takes two halves: for every \(s\in\{1,2,3\}\) and every \(n,A,b,x_0\) as above, every nonterminating orbit has \((y_{2k})_{k\ge0}\) converging in Euclidean norm, and for \(s=4\) there exist \(n,A,b,x_0\) as above such that \(r_k\ne0\) for every \(k\ge0\) but \((y_{2k})_{k\ge0}\) does not converge in Euclidean norm. Split the halves, and the pieces of each, between you on Slack.

Stop only when the team's proof is complete. Before you stop, post on Slack what you proved, and what is left.

[tool call: write_scratchpad]
content:
```
import numpy as np
rng=np.random.default_rng(312)
def orth(l,w,s):
    V=np.vander(l,s+1,increasing=True)
    c=np.linalg.solve(V[:,:s].T@(w[:,None]*V[:,:s]),-(V[:,:s].T@(w*V[:,s])))
    p=V@np.r_[c,1.]
    return p,np.dot(w,p*p)
for s in [2,3,4]:
    mins=np.ones(s)*1e9;maxs=-mins
    for z in range(1000):
        l=np.sort(rng.uniform(-1,1,2*s+3));w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(len(l)))
        p,h=orth(l,w,s); wn=w*p*p/h
        dif=np.array([orth(l,wn,j)[1]-orth(l,w,j)[1] for j in range(1,s+1)])
        mins=np.minimum(mins,dif);maxs=np.maximum(maxs,dif)
    print(s,mins,maxs)

```

from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as Poly
for s in [2,3,4]:
    pp=Poly.fromroots(np.linspace(-.8,.8,s))
    # small level ensures 2s simple roots
    h=min(abs(pp(z)) for z in pp.deriv().roots())**2/4
    l=np.sort((pp*pp-h).roots())
    w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(len(l)))
    last=None
    for k in range(100001):
        p,hs=orth(l,w,s);w=w*p*p/hs
        if k in [100,1000,10000,100000]:
            print(s,k,'hs/h',hs/h,'w',np.round(w,5),'delta',None if last is None else np.linalg.norm(w-last));last=w.copy()

```

from pathlib import Path
script = r'''"""Exact structural checks for restarted CG normalized residuals.
Run with Python/SymPy. No floating-point arithmetic is used.

import sympy as S
z=S.Symbol('z')
def op(nodes,w,s):
    G=S.Matrix([[sum(wi*xi**(j+k) for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w)) for k in range(s)] for j in range(s)])
    rhs=S.Matrix([-sum(wi*xi**(j+s) for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w)) for j in range(s)])
    c=G.inv()*rhs
    q=z**s+sum(c[j]*z**j for j in range(s))
    h=S.factor(sum(wi*q.subs(z,xi)**2 for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w)))
    return q,h
def step(nodes,w,s):
    q,h=op(nodes,w,s)
    return [S.factor(wi*q.subs(z,xi)**2/h) for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w)],q,h
for s in (1,2,3,4):
    nodes=list(map(S.Rational,range(1,2*s+1)))
    raw=[S.Rational((i+2)**2+1) for i in range(2*s)]
    w=[v/sum(raw) for v in raw]
    wp,q,h=step(nodes,w,s);wpp,qp,hp=step(nodes,wp,s)
    prod=S.expand(q*qp)
    assert sum(wi*prod.subs(z,xi) for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w))==h
    variance=S.factor(sum(wi*(prod.subs(z,xi)-h)**2 for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w)))
    assert variance==h*(hp-h) and hp>=h
    # Rational Stieltjes numerator and remainder factorization, n=2s.
    P=S.prod(z-xi for xi in nodes)
    M=S.expand(sum(wi*S.div(P,z-xi,z)[0] for xi,wi in zip(nodes,w)))
    Mp=S.expand(sum(wi*S.div(P,z-xi,z)[0] for xi,wi in zip(nodes,wp)))
    rem=S.rem(q*M,P,z)
    assert S.degree(rem,z)<=s-1
    assert S.Poly(rem,z).LC()==h
    assert S.expand(Mp-q*rem/h)==0
print('PASS: exact Lyapunov and Stieltjes-numerator identities for s=1,2,3,4')
# Exact feature formulation for p0(z)=z^2-5 at roots p0=+-4.
# Four positive, distinct eigenvalues after an immaterial shift if needed.
nodes=list(map(S.Rational,[-3,-1,1,3]));p0=z*z-5;h0=S.Integer(16);s=2
w=list(map(S.Rational,[S.Rational(1,10),S.Rational(2,10),S.Rational(3,10),S.Rational(4,10)]))
a=[S.Matrix([1,xi])/p0.subs(z,xi) for xi in nodes]
m=sum((wi*ai for wi,ai in zip(w,a)),S.zeros(s,1))
G=sum((wi*ai*ai.T for wi,ai in zip(w,a)),S.zeros(s))
u=G.inv()*m;d=(m.T*u)[0]
wp,q,h=step(nodes,w,s)
assert S.expand(q-(p0-sum(u[j]*z**j for j in range(s)))))==0
assert h==h0*(1-d)
for wi,wpi,ai in zip(w,wp,a):
    assert wpi==S.factor(wi*(1-(ai.T*u)[0])**2/(1-d))
Huu=sum((wi*ai*(ai.T*u)[0]**2 for wi,ai in zip(w,a)),S.zeros(s,1))
mp=sum((wi*ai for wi,ai in zip(wp,a)),S.zeros(s,1))
assert mp==(-m+Huu)/(1-d)
print('PASS: exact p0^2=h0 feature reduction and normal-moment map')
'''
# Fix a deliberately isolated parenthesis in generated assertion.

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
p=P([0,-3,0,1]); nodes=np.sort((p*p-1).roots()); s=3
V=np.vander(nodes,4,increasing=True)
def orth(w):
    c=np.linalg.solve(V[:,:3].T@(w[:,None]*V[:,:3]),-V[:,:3].T@(w*V[:,3]));q=P(np.r_[c,1]);h=w@q(nodes)**2
    return q,h
for d in [.1,.03,.01]:
    roots1=np.sort((p-d).roots());roots2=np.sort((p-2*d).roots());q=P.fromroots([roots1[0],roots1[1],roots2[2]])
    C=np.vstack([np.ones(6)]+[q(nodes)*nodes**j for j in range(3)]);v=np.r_[1.,0,0,0]
    w0=np.ones(6)/6;w=w0+C.T@np.linalg.solve(C@C.T,v-C@w0)
    q0,h=orth(w)
    for _ in range(2):
        q,h=orth(w);w=w*q(nodes)**2/h
    q2,h=orth(w)
    a=p(np.sort(q2.roots()));print(d,a,'diffpair',a[0]-a[1], 'mind',min(w))

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
from scipy.optimize import linprog
x=P([0,1]);q=(x*x-1)*(x-2)*(x+3);r=(x*x-1)*(x+2)*(x-3);pp=q*r-1
l=np.sort(pp.roots());print('nodes',l)
V=np.vander(l,4,increasing=True);qi=q(l);ri=r(l)
# maximize common minimum weight
C=np.vstack([np.ones(8),(qi[:,None]*V).T]);rhs=np.r_[1.,np.zeros(4)]
res=linprog(np.r_[np.zeros(8),-1],A_ub=np.c_[-np.eye(8),np.ones(8)],b_ub=np.zeros(8),A_eq=np.c_[C,np.zeros(5)],b_eq=rhs,bounds=[(None,None)]*9,method='highs')
w=res.x[:8];print('weight',w,'min',min(w));wp=w*qi**2
print('norm prime',sum(wp),'orthprime',V.T@(wp*ri))
def jac(w,qv):
    G=V.T@(w[:,None]*V);h=w@qv**2
    return np.diag(qv*qv/h)-2/h*np.diag(w*qv)@V@np.linalg.inv(G)@V.T@np.diag(qv)-np.outer(w*qv*qv,qv*qv)/(h*h)
J=jac(wp,ri)@jac(w,qi)
print('eigenvals',np.linalg.eigvals(J))
print('rank J-I',np.linalg.svd(J-np.eye(8))[1]);print('eig one ranks',np.linalg.svd((J-np.eye(8))@(J-np.eye(8)))[1])
np.savez('workspace/agent3_gcd2_candidate.npz',nodes=l,w=w,q=q.coef,r=r.coef,J=J)

```

from pathlib import Path
Path('workspace/agent3_global_reduction.md').write_text(r'''# Global reduction for every restart length

Work on the distinct eigenvalues initially present in the residual. Their spectral residual directions are fixed, so write the normalized residual using signed scalar coefficients y_i and weights w_i=y_i^2. All eigenvalues below are distinct, and initially all w_i>0. Let q_w be the monic degree-s orthogonal polynomial for w and h(w)=sum_i w_i q_w(lambda_i)^2. Nontermination implies support size>s. The normalized residual map is T y=(-1)^s q_w(A)y/sqrt(h(w)); its weight map is T_i(w)=w_i q_w(lambda_i)^2/h(w). Once an initial component vanishes it may simply be deleted.

For q=q_w and r=q_Tw, orthogonality gives E_w[qr]=h(w), and

    h(w) (h(Tw)-h(w)) = E_w[(qr-h(w))^2] >=0.

Consequently h_k increases to h_infinity>0. The compact region h>=h_0 stays away from moment-matrix singularities: a singular degree-(s-1) moment matrix would have <=s-1 support points and hence h=0. Therefore q,T,h are continuous there. Also

    <y,T^2 y> = sqrt(h(w)/h(Tw)),

so T^2 y-y ->0. Every cluster point is a two-cycle, and on its support qr=h_infinity. Its support therefore has at most2s points. The omega-limit set of even iterates is connected, by asymptotic regularity and compactness.

There is a family of additional Lyapunovs. For any real z below every eigenvalue define

    v_i = [w_i/(lambda_i-z)] / Z_z(w),
    p(t)=q_w(t)/q_w(z),
    Gamma_z(w)^2 = sum_i v_i p(lambda_i)^2.

The polynomial p minimizes this norm among degree<=s polynomials satisfying p(z)=1: any admissible variation is (t-z)a(t), deg a<s, and its inner product with p is a nonzero constant times E_w[q_w a]=0. Its normalized residual map is precisely the same weight map, with w replaced by v. If p_next=q_Tw/q_Tw(z), then the variational identity gives

    E_v[p p_next]=E_v[p^2]=Gamma_z(w)^2,
    E_v[(p p_next)^2]=Gamma_z(w)^2 Gamma_z(Tw)^2.

Cauchy-Schwarz yields Gamma_z(Tw)>=Gamma_z(w). Also Gamma_z<=1 by admissibility of the constant polynomial1. Thus Gamma_z converges. On a limiting two-cycle qr=h_infinity, its Cauchy-Schwarz equality implies

    Gamma_z(infinity)^2 = h_infinity/[q(z) r(z)].

Choose2s distinct z below the spectrum. The monic degree-2s polynomial Q=qr is therefore the SAME at all even cluster points. Each q is a monic degree-s divisor of Q, and there are finitely many such divisors. Connectedness forces a single q, and similarly a single r. Hence q_even ->q and q_odd ->r for every s, with qr=Q.

For every initially present node, the limiting two-block residual multiplier is Q(lambda_i)/h_infinity. Boundedness of normalized residual coordinates implies |Q(lambda_i)|<=h_infinity: if its modulus exceeded1, a nonzero component would grow geometrically, impossible. Define S={i:Q(lambda_i)=h_infinity}. For i outsideS, the variance identity has a limiting nonzero squared factor at that node, so sum_k w_{k,i}<infinity. In particular these weights vanish. Nodes with |Q(lambda_i)|<h_infinity actually vanish exponentially; the only possibly non-exponential outside modes satisfy Q(lambda_i)=-h_infinity.

Every even omega-limit is a point of the SINGLE convex polytope

    W={w>=0: support(w) subsetS, sum w=1,
                sum_i w_i q(lambda_i)lambda_i^j=0, 0<=j<s}.

On W the limiting weight map is w_i ->w_i q(lambda_i)^2/h_infinity, and its square is the identity. The polytope dimension is at most s-1, because |S|<=2s and the s+1 constraints are independent on nonempty faces with h>0. If even weights converge, then even normalized residual vectors converge as well: on each limiting positive-weight component the two-block multiplier tends+1, so its sign is eventually fixed; all other components vanish.

Thus the remaining problem is convergence (s<=3) or nonconvergence (s4) of weights near one fixed polytope W, not wandering orthogonal-polynomial coefficients. The above reduction alone does NOT prove convergence of weights.
''')
exec(Path('workspace/agent3_structural_checks.py').read_text())

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
rng=np.random.default_rng(2025)
def oth(l,w,s):
    V=np.vander(l,s+1,increasing=True);c=np.linalg.solve(V[:,:s].T@(w[:,None]*V[:,:s]),-V[:,:s].T@(w*V[:,s]));q=P(np.r_[c,1]);h=w@q(l)**2;return q,h
for s in [3,4,5]:
    bad=0;outside=0;raw=[]

        l=np.sort(rng.uniform(-1,1,2*s));w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(2*s));poly=P.fromroots(l);M=sum([w[i]*(poly//P([-li,1])) for i,li in enumerate(l)],P([0]));q,h=oth(l,w,s);wp=w*q(l)**2/h;qp,hp=oth(l,wp,s);Mp=sum([wp[i]*(poly//P([-li,1])) for i,li in enumerate(l)],P([0]));a,t=divmod(q*M,poly);ap,tp=divmod(qp*Mp,poly);B,rem=divmod(a*tp-ap*t,q);roots=B.trim(tol=1e-9).roots();

    print(s,'nonreal',bad,'outside',outside,'examples',raw)

from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
rng=np.random.default_rng(2026)
def oth(l,w,s):
    V=np.vander(l,s+1,increasing=True);c=np.linalg.solve(V[:,:s].T@(w[:,None]*V[:,:s]),-V[:,:s].T@(w*V[:,s]));q=P(np.r_[c,1]);h=w@q(l)**2;return q,h
p=P([0,-3,0,1]);l=np.sort((p*p-1).roots());poly=p*p-1

        w=np.ones(6)/6+eps*rng.uniform(-1,1,6);w/=sum(w)

Since q_{k+2} is orthogonal for w_{k+2}, the coefficient vector of q_{k+2}-q_k is obtained by multiplying the negative right hand side by the inverse s-by-s moment Gram matrix of w_{k+2}. Thus even and odd monic-polynomial coefficients have bounded total variation and converge, for EVERY s. This identity has been checked exactly for rational examples s1,...,4 in agent3_structural_checks.py. It does NOT by itself prove convergence of residual weights.\n'''
Path('workspace/agent3_global_reduction.md').write_text(proof)

from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
x=P([0,1]);alpha=1.;beta=13/8;gamma=7/4
q=(x*x-1)*(x-beta)*(x+gamma);r=(x*x-1)*(x+beta)*(x-gamma);H=10125/65536;Q=q*r;poly=Q-H;l=np.sort(poly.roots());extras=np.array([-np.sqrt(23/8),np.sqrt(23/8)])
print('nodes',l,'extras',extras)
V=np.vander(l,4,increasing=True);fext=np.vander(extras,4,increasing=True);qv=q(l);rv=r(l)
# fixedvertices choose core spectralindices0,7 and1from each ofpairs(1,2),(3,4),(5,6)
vertices=[]
for bits in itertools.product([0,1],repeat=3):
    ix=[0,1+bits[0],3+bits[1],5+bits[2],7];C=np.vstack([np.ones(5),(qv[ix,None]*V[ix]).T]);w=np.zeros(8);w[ix]=np.linalg.solve(C,np.r_[1.,np.zeros(4)])
    assert min(w)>-1e-9
    G=V.T@(w[:,None]*V);wp=w*qv*qv/H;Gp=V.T@(wp[:,None]*V)
    # coeff dr=-invGp G*v; deltaF functional allnodes andextras

    deh=w@Emap; Emap-=deh
    # Atvertex E shouldzero onactive

    drmap=-np.linalg.solve(Gp,G)
    Fext=r(extras)[:,None]*fext+q(extras)[:,None]*fext@drmap
    # force v=Cforce eta
    Cf=4*np.linalg.solve(G,q(extras)[:,None]*fext).T if False else 4*np.linalg.solve(G,(q(extras)[:,None]*fext).T)
    L=(Fext+deh)@Cf/H
    eta=np.linalg.solve(L,np.ones(2));v=Cf@eta
    growth=2*Emap@v/H
    # Edge coordinate flips missingnodegrowth
    gr=[growth[1+(1-bits[0])],growth[3+(1-bits[1])],growth[5+(1-bits[2])]]
    print(bits,'minw',min(w[ix]),'eta',eta,'edge',np.round(gr,5))
    vertices.append((bits,eta,gr))

from pathlib import Path
code=r'''import numpy as np,itertools
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
x=P([0,1]);beta=13/8;gamma=7/4
q=(x*x-1)*(x-beta)*(x+gamma);r=(x*x-1)*(x+beta)*(x-gamma);H=10125/65536;Q=q*r;poly=Q-H;l=np.sort(poly.roots());extras=np.array([-np.sqrt(23/8),np.sqrt(23/8)])
V=np.vander(l,4,increasing=True);fext=np.vander(extras,4,increasing=True);qv=q(l);rv=r(l)
for bits in itertools.product([0,1],repeat=3):
    ix=[0,1+bits[0],3+bits[1],5+bits[2],7]
    A=np.vstack([np.ones(5),(qv[ix,None]*V[ix]).T]);w=np.zeros(8);w[ix]=np.linalg.solve(A,np.r_[1.,np.zeros(4)])
    assert min(w)>-1e-9
    G=V.T@(w[:,None]*V);wp=w*qv*qv/H;Gp=V.T@(wp[:,None]*V)
    drmap=-np.linalg.solve(Gp,G)
    Fmap=rv[:,None]*V+qv[:,None]*V@drmap
    deh=w@Fmap;Emap=Fmap-deh
    assert np.max(np.abs(Emap[ix]))<1e-5
    Fext=r(extras)[:,None]*fext+q(extras)[:,None]*fext@drmap
    Cf=4*np.linalg.solve(G,(q(extras)[:,None]*fext).T)
    L=(Fext+deh)@Cf/H
    eta=np.linalg.solve(L,np.ones(2));v=Cf@eta
    growth=2*Emap@v/H
    gr=[growth[1+(1-bits[0])],growth[3+(1-bits[1])],growth[5+(1-bits[2])]]
    print(bits,'eta',eta,'edge',np.round(gr,5))
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_negative_vertices.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent3_negative_vertices.py','exec'))

```

import numpy as np,itertools
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
x=P([0,1]);q=(x*x-1)*(x-2);r=(x*x-1)*(x-3);H=.1;Q=q*r;poly=Q-H;l=np.sort(poly.roots());alphas=[-1,1];L=np.array([[poly(a)/((a-li)*poly.deriv()(li)) for li in l] for a in alphas]);print('nodes',l,'L',L)
for iz,jz in itertools.product([1,2],[3,4]):
    v=L[0,[iz,jz]]-L[1,[iz,jz]]
    ts=np.array([v[1],-v[0]])
    if max(ts)<=0:ts=-ts
    if min(ts)>=0:
        val=L[0,[iz,jz]]@ts
        print('zeros',iz+1,jz+1,'rates',ts,'commonlogcoef',-val,'FEASIBLE',val<0)
    else:print('zeros',iz+1,jz+1,'no equalrates')

```

from pathlib import Path
code=r'''from sage.all import *
R=PolynomialRing(QQ,'z');z=R.gen();p=z**3-7*z;Q=p*p-25;H=QQ(11);P=Q-H
nodes=list(map(QQ,[-3,-2,-1,1,2,3]));assert P==prod(z-a for a in nodes)
neg=sorted((Q+H).roots(AA,multiplicities=False));beta=neg[0];gamma=neg[-1]
assert -3<beta<-2 and 2<gamma<3
for index in [2,3]:
    xi=nodes[index]
    aa=(beta-xi)/(beta-gamma);bb=(gamma-xi)/(gamma-beta)
    assert aa>0 and bb>0 and aa+bb==1
    coeff=[]
    for li in nodes:
        Lb=AA(P(beta))/((beta-li)*P.derivative()(li))
        Lg=AA(P(gamma))/((gamma-li)*P.derivative()(li))
        coeff.append(aa*Lb+bb*Lg)
    assert coeff[index]==0
    other=3 if index==2 else 2
    assert coeff[other]<0
    assert all(coeff[j]>=0 for j in range(6) if j!=other)
print('PASS: exact algebraic two-outer-band entropy certificates isolate BOTH middle endpoints')
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_s3_entropy_middle_check.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent3_s3_entropy_middle_check.py','exec'))

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
rng=np.random.default_rng(2026)
best=(0,None)
for z in range(100000):
    # chooseq/rrootsoneperbandbyrandomwell-separatedpairs
    centers=np.sort(rng.uniform(-2,2,3));widths=rng.uniform(.005,.2,3)
    if min(np.diff(centers))<.5:continue
    qr=[]
    for c,d in zip(centers,widths): qr.extend([c-d,c+d])
    Q=P.fromroots(qr)
    crit=Q.deriv().roots();pos=[Q(c) for c in crit if abs(c.imag)<1e-9 and Q(c).real>1e-12]
    neg=[-Q(c) for c in crit if abs(c.imag)<1e-9 and Q(c).real< -1e-12]
    if not pos or not neg:continue
    H=min(min(pos),max(neg))*rng.uniform(.01,.99)
    l=np.sort((Q-H).roots())
    if max(abs(l.imag))>1e-8:continue
    l=l.real;pp=Q-H
    for beta in (Q+H).roots():
        if abs(beta.imag)>1e-8:continue
        beta=beta.real;Ls=pp(beta)/((beta-l)*pp.deriv()(l))
        a=-min(Ls)
        if a>best[0]:best=(a,(qr,H,l,beta,Ls))
    if best[0]>1:break
print('best',best)

```

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
rng=np.random.default_rng(2026);best=(0,None);ct=0
for z in range(100000):
    cen=np.sort(rng.uniform(-2,2,3));wid=rng.uniform(.005,.3,3)
    if min(np.diff(cen))<.6:continue
    Q=P.fromroots(np.ravel([[c-d,c+d] for c,d in zip(cen,wid)]));crit=Q.deriv().roots()
    pos=[Q(c).real for c in crit if abs(c.imag)<1e-9 and Q(c).real>1e-10]
    neg=[-Q(c).real for c in crit if abs(c.imag)<1e-9 and Q(c).real< -1e-10]
    if not pos or not neg:continue
    H=min(min(pos),max(neg))*rng.uniform(.01,.99);pp=Q-H;l=np.sort(pp.roots())
    if max(abs(l.imag))>1e-8:continue
    l=l.real;ex=[b.real for b in (Q+H).roots() if abs(b.imag)<1e-8]
    b1=[b for b in ex if l[0]<b<l[1]];b2=[b for b in ex if l[2]<b<l[3]]
    if not b1 or not b2:continue
    for beta in b1:
      for gamma in b2:
       for xi in [l[1],l[2]]:
        aa=(beta-xi)/(beta-gamma);bb=1-aa
        Lb=pp(beta)/((beta-l)*pp.deriv()(l));Lg=pp(gamma)/((gamma-l)*pp.deriv()(l));c=aa*Lb+bb*Lg
        val=-c[4];ct+=1
        if val>best[0]:best=(val,(Q.coef,H,l,beta,gamma,xi,c))
    if best[0]>1:break
print('ct',ct,'best',best)

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
rng=np.random.default_rng(2026);best=(0,None)
for z in range(200000):
 cen=np.sort(rng.uniform(-2,2,3));wid=rng.uniform(.001,.4,3)
 if min(np.diff(cen))<.2:continue
 Q=P.fromroots(np.ravel([[c-d,c+d] for c,d in zip(cen,wid)]));crit=Q.deriv().roots();pos=[Q(c).real for c in crit if abs(c.imag)<1e-9 and Q(c).real>1e-10];neg=[-Q(c).real for c in crit if abs(c.imag)<1e-9 and Q(c).real< -1e-10]
 if not pos or not neg:continue
 H=min(min(pos),max(neg))*rng.uniform(.001,.999);pp=Q-H;l=np.sort(pp.roots())
 if max(abs(l.imag))>1e-8:continue
 l=l.real
 for beta in (Q+H).roots():
  if abs(beta.imag)>1e-8 or not l[0]<beta.real<l[1]:continue
  beta=beta.real;a=-pp(beta)/((beta-l[4])*pp.deriv()(l[4]))
  if a>best[0]:best=(a,(Q.coef,H,l,beta))
 if best[0]>1:break
print('best',best)

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
l=np.array([-8,-7,-4,-1,1,4,7,8],float)/8
p=P([424/4096,0,-65/64,0,1]);sgn=np.sign(p(l));groupl=[l[sgn>0],l[sgn<0]]
f=[np.vander(x,4,increasing=True)[:,1:] for x in groupl];m=f[0].mean(0);assert np.linalg.norm(m-f[1].mean(0))<1e-10
C=[];Is=[];Ts=[]
for ff in f:
 a=ff-m;cc=a.T@a/4;C.append(cc);Is.append(np.linalg.inv(cc));Ts.append(np.einsum('ni,nj,nk->ijk',a,a,a)/4)
A,B=Is;Cp,Cm=C;Delta=A-B;ev,U=np.linalg.eigh(Delta);print('inertia',ev)
for d in [.1,.5,.9]:
 S=(1-d)*A+(1+d)*B;N=(1-d)*Cp+(1+d)*Cm
 for k in range(4):
  # make nullvector with1positivecoordand2negativecoords
  cn=np.array([np.cos(k),np.sin(k),1.]);pp=U@(cn/np.sqrt(abs(ev)))
  da=-A@np.einsum('ijk,j,k->i',Ts[0],A@pp,A@pp)
  db=-B@np.einsum('ijk,j,k->i',Ts[1],B@pp,B@pp)
  aa=-np.linalg.solve(S,(1-d)*da+(1+d)*db+2*d*np.linalg.solve(N,Cp@da-Cm@db))
  ag=-.5*np.linalg.solve(Delta,da-db)
  dif=aa-ag
  print(d,k,'null',pp@Delta@pp,'diffperp',np.linalg.norm(dif-pp*(pp@dif)/(pp@pp)),'diff',np.linalg.norm(dif))

from pathlib import Path
Path('workspace/agent3_s3_gcd2_lemma.md').write_text(r'''# All-dimensional s=3 convergence when the limiting monic polynomials have gcd degree2

This lemma uses the team's proved global reduction and bounded-variation tail estimate. Write the limiting even and odd monic cubics as q,r, put Q=qr, and H=lim h_k>0. Assume gcd(q,r)=c is monic of degree2. Its roots alpha1,alpha2 are distinct real roots, since each individual limiting cubic remains a degree3 orthogonal polynomial for a measure with positive monic norm H. Let D_k=H-h_k.

The global BV identity gives

    ||q_k-q_parity|| <= C D_k.

Let S be the union of supports of the even omega-limit points. Every node in S satisfies Q(lambda)=H. If |S|<=4, its weights are uniquely determined by sum w=1 and the three orthogonality equations for q, so convergence is immediate. It remains to consider p=|S|=5 or6. Let P0=prod_{lambda in S}(z-lambda) and define the monic polynomial L by

    Q-H=P0 L,      deg L=6-p.

All initially retained nodes outside S with |Q(lambda)|<H decay exponentially and may be projected away; every smooth algebraic identity below then has exponentially small error. Such errors do not affect the estimates or the one-sided variation argument below. The remaining external nodes beta satisfy Q(beta)=-H. Their total mass eta_k is summable. None is a root of q or r, and none lies in a limiting Dirichlet gap. Put E(z)=prod_external(z-beta).

Let M_k/P0E be the Stieltjes transform of the retained weights. Perform polynomial division

    q_k M_k = a_k P0 E + t_k.

Here a_k is monic quadratic. The s=3 orthogonality relations give deg t_k=p+deg E-4 and leading coefficient h_k. Split its roots into the p-4 persistent Dirichlet roots and the external roots:

    t_k=h_k tau_k U_k,
    deg tau_k=p-4,  U_k=E+O(eta_k).

The estimate for U follows from smoothness of simple Stieltjes-remainder roots: setting an external weight to0 gives an exact factor z-beta, and its persistent Dirichlet roots remain uniformly separated from the beta's. All limiting measures have positive3-by3 moment Gram matrix, uniformly on the compact omega-limit set. Consequently the roots of a_k remain simple, and persistent roots match distinct roots of a_k. At limits,

    a=L tau,       M=r tau E.

The persistent tau roots lie in gaps not occupied by q or r roots; this follows from interlacing of M=r tau E with the core nodes and from qr=H on those nodes.

Define the quadratic normal polynomial

    N_k=H a_k-h_k L tau_k.

Its leading coefficient is D_k, up to the exponentially small projection error.

For either common root alpha of q,r one has M_k(alpha)=O(D_k+eta_k). Indeed on every core node,

    1/(alpha-lambda)=-q(lambda)[r(z)/(z-alpha)]_{z=lambda}/H.

The polynomial r(z)/(z-alpha) has degree2, so orthogonality of q_k and the BV tail bound show that the core Stieltjes transform at alpha is O(D_k+eta_k); the external contribution is O(eta_k). Since q_k(alpha)=O(D_k), polynomial division, evaluated at alpha, gives

    N_k(alpha)=O(D_k^2+eta_k).

In detail, P0(alpha)L(alpha)=-H, and division yields

    H a_k(alpha)-h_k L(alpha)tau_k(alpha)
      =-L(alpha)q_k(alpha)M_k(alpha)/E(alpha)
        +h_k L(alpha)tau_k(alpha)(U_k(alpha)/E(alpha)-1).

All denominators are nonzero and uniformly bounded away from0, because negative-level external nodes cannot equal a common root.

A quadratic with leading coefficient D_k whose values at alpha1,alpha2 are O(D_k^2+eta_k) must satisfy

    N_k(z)=D_k c(z)+O(D_k^2+eta_k)

coefficientwise.

Let zeta_j be a persistent root of tau_k, and eta_j the matched root of a_k. Write R_j=zeta_j-eta_j. If a_k=(z-eta_j)A_j, then

    R_j=N_k(zeta_j)/(H A_j(zeta_j))
       =D_k c(zeta_j)/(H A_j(zeta_j))+O(D_k^2+eta_k).

The coefficient c(zeta_j)/(H A_j(zeta_j)) has a fixed sign and modulus bounded away from0 uniformly near the limiting polytope: c has roots only in the q/r bands, not in persistent Dirichlet gaps, and A_j has roots uniformly separated from zeta_j. Thus the wrong-sign part of R_j is bounded by C eta_k plus an exponentially small error; the O(D_k^2) term is absorbed into the leading nonzero multiple of D_k.

Finally the exact new-pole evaluation identity from the team's compression/pole analysis gives

    zeta_{j,k+1}-zeta_{j,k}=C_{j,k} R_{j,k+1},
    0<=C_{j,k}<=C,

up to exponentially small projection error. Therefore the wrong-direction variation of each persistent pole is summable. A bounded real sequence with summable variation in one direction converges. All persistent poles converge; hence tau_k, M_k, the weights, and finally the even normalized residual vectors converge.

This proves the gcd-degree2 case for s=3 in arbitrary dimension. The argument is quantitative and does not invoke a generic summable-noise theorem.
''')
print('Wrote gcd2 lemma')

```

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
l=np.array([-8,-7,-4,-1,1,4,7,8],float)/8
pol=P([424/4096,0,-65/64,0,1]);sgn=np.sign(pol(l));gl=[l[sgn>0],l[sgn<0]]
f=[np.vander(x,4,increasing=True)[:,1:] for x in gl];m=f[0].mean(0)
C=[];Is=[];Ts=[]
for ff in f:
 a=ff-m;cc=a.T@a/4;C.append(cc);Is.append(np.linalg.inv(cc));Ts.append(np.einsum('ni,nj,nk->ijk',a,a,a)/4)
A,B=Is;Cp,Cm=C
for d in [.1,.5,.9]:
 H=1-d*d;S=(1-d)*A+(1+d)*B;N=(1-d)*Cp+(1+d)*Cm;D=(1+d)*Cp+(1-d)*Cm;Di=np.linalg.inv(D)
 Jx=.5*(N-N@Di@N);J=(H/(8*d))**2*S@Jx@S
 dCs=[];dIs=[]
 for cc,ii,T in zip(C,Is,Ts):
  dc=np.array([np.einsum('ijk,k->ij',T,ii[:,a]) for a in range(3)]);dCs.append(dc);dIs.append(np.array([-ii@dc[a]@ii for a in range(3)]))
 dj=[]
 for a in range(3):
  dn=(1-d)*dCs[0][a]+(1+d)*dCs[1][a];dd=(1+d)*dCs[0][a]+(1-d)*dCs[1][a];ds=(1-d)*dIs[0][a]+(1+d)*dIs[1][a]
  dx=.5*(dn-dn@Di@N-N@Di@dn+N@Di@dd@Di@N)
  dj.append((H/(8*d))**2*(ds@Jx@S+S@dx@S+S@Jx@ds))
 dj=np.array(dj);ev,U=np.linalg.eigh(J);print('d',d,'inertia',ev)
 for k in range(4):
  # signature 1neg2pos or2neg1pos adapt
  indsneg=np.where(ev<0)[0];indspos=np.where(ev>0)[0];cn=np.zeros(3)
  many=indsneg if len(indsneg)==2 else indspos;one=indspos if len(indspos)==1 else indsneg
  cn[many]=[np.cos(k),np.sin(k)];cn[one]=1
  v=U@(cn/np.sqrt(abs(ev)))
  da=-A@np.einsum('ijk,j,k->i',Ts[0],A@v,A@v);db=-B@np.einsum('ijk,j,k->i',Ts[1],B@v,B@v)
  aa=-np.linalg.solve(S,(1-d)*da+(1+d)*db+2*d*np.linalg.solve(N,Cp@da-Cm@db))
  dJv=np.einsum('a,aij->ij',v,dj);gradE=np.einsum('i,aij,j->a',v,dj,v)
  ag=-np.linalg.solve(J,dJv@v-.5*gradE)
  dif=aa-ag;perp=np.linalg.norm(dif-v*(v@dif)/(v@v))
  print('k',k,'null',v@J@v,'diffperp',perp,'denergy',2*v@J@aa+v@dJv@v)

content:
```
from pathlib import Path
Path('workspace/agent3_s3_global_cases.md').write_text(r'''# s=3 global case reduction: proved cases and precise remaining step

Notation follows agent3_global_reduction.md and agent1_global_polynomial_proof.md. The even and odd monic cubics converge with bounded total variation to q,r; H=lim h>0; Q=qr. After discarding a finite initial segment all retained spectral coordinates are nonzero. Let Splus be ALL retained nodes with Q(lambda)=H (not merely the union of limiting positive-weight supports). Off Splus, weights are summable. Those with |Q|<H are exponentially small, while the only remaining external nodes satisfy Q(beta)=-H.

## Already proved

1. If |Splus|<=4 then the limiting weights are unique from the three q-orthogonality equations and normalization, hence convergence.

2. If q=r, convergence for every dimension follows from agent2_equal_limit_polynomials.md. BV-tail distance of q_k from q is O(H-h_k), while the projection identity makes it quadratic in that distance plus exponentially small external weights; absorption gives exponential convergence.

3. If deg gcd(q,r)=2, convergence for every dimension follows from agent3_s3_gcd2_lemma.md, independently audited by agent1 (#267). The quadratic normal polynomial is D*c+O(D^2+eta), so persistent residues have fixed signs modulo summable wrong-sign parts.

4. If there are NO negative-level external nodes, all external weights decay exponentially. Project onto the at-most-six core nodes. Exact core pole recurrences plus exponentially small perturbations prove convergence, as follows.
   - Five-node core: a is monic quadratic and t=h(z-zeta). The scalar residue R=zeta-eta satisfies R_next=C R, C->1 positive, by the constant-B determinant identity. Exponentially small errors are handled by the scalar integrating-factor lemma.
   - Six-node core: a and t/h are monic quadratics. Each residue has the exact recurrence R_j,next=C_j R_j-D_j K, with C_j->1 positive, D_1>0 and D_2<0; K=h_next-h>=0. The same integrating-factor lemma gives eventual sign or an exponentially small wrong-sign part. B-compression/interlacing gives the nonnegative bounded new-pole coefficient, so both poles converge.
   See agent2_B_compression_proof.md and the team's exact pole recurrences.

## Entropies for negative external nodes

For a full simple six-node core lambda1<...<lambda6, let ell_i be its Lagrange interpolation polynomials. For Q(beta)=-H,

    L_beta=log w_beta + sum_i ell_i(beta) log w_i

is eventually strictly increasing, with increment at least c(H-h_k). BV-tail control makes every Taylor remainder O((H-h_k)^2), whereas the leading term is positive of order H-h_k. If beta is in band B_j=(lambda_{2j-1},lambda_{2j}), its negative core coefficients are:

    B1: i=3,5;     B2: i=2,5;     B3: i=2,4.

Thus any negative external node forces the omega-limit set onto a proper union of two adjacent edges of the fixed square. Negative external nodes cannot occupy all three bands (positive Stieltjes/entropy-cone certificate, agent1 #198).

### Two occupied outer bands: convergence is proved

Suppose beta in B1 and gamma in B3. Let xi=lambda3 or lambda4 and take positive a,b with

    a/(z-beta)+b/(z-gamma)=(z-xi)/((z-beta)(z-gamma)).

Then c_i=a ell_i(beta)+b ell_i(gamma) equals

    2H(lambda_i-xi)/[(lambda_i-beta)(lambda_i-gamma)P'(lambda_i)].

For xi=lambda3, all c_i are nonnegative except c_4<0 (c_3=0). Monotonicity of aL_beta+bL_gamma and w_beta,w_gamma->0 therefore force w_4->0. Choosing xi=lambda4 forces w_3->0. Only four core nodes remain, so the limiting weights are unique. An exact algebraic instance of both certificates is checked in agent3_s3_entropy_middle_check.py.

### One occupied middle band: convergence is proved by extremal poles

If negative external nodes occur only in B2, the two persistent Dirichlet poles are respectively the leftmost and rightmost non-exponentially-decaying t-poles. At a zero residue, B has that pole as a root; interlacing with the next t polynomial makes residue crossings one-way. Regularity holds for gcd<=1, while gcd2 is handled independently above. The one-sided scalar integrating-factor argument, with exponentially small discarded modes, then gives convergence of both poles.

### Outer empty band: exact remaining geometry

Suppose the only possible occupied bands are B1,B2 and B3 is empty. A positive two-node Stieltjes certificate with numerator z-lambda2 or z-lambda3 has all nonnegative core coefficients except c5<0. Therefore w5->0, confining the omega-limit set to the fixed edge w5=0. The right persistent pole tends lambda5 and has bounded variation by its extremal-pole barrier. If only B1 is occupied, the same conclusion is needed only when that extremal pole tends its outward endpoint lambda5; other limiting positions force the second pole to an endpoint via the single entropy and yield convergence directly. The reflected configuration is identical.

On the edge, let u be the remaining persistent pole, let g=w5, and let R1,R2 be the two residues. Put D=H-h. The proved BV estimate gives |R_j|<=C D. Analytic-ideal factorization yields, for the exact six-node core,

    R1,next = a_k R1 - g R2^2 B_k,       B_k>=0,

after absorbing terms divisible by R1 into a_k. Negative external nodes add signed linear forcing: the far band B1 has positive coefficient and the nearer B2 negative coefficient. On every compact OPEN edge segment, entropy comparison shows the near-band mass is a higher power of the far-band mass, hence is negligible. The ghost forcing gR2^2 has tail o(D), because g is comparable to the outward pole's remaining distance and that pole is monotone.

The team has established pole-energy and residue-BV lemmas (agent2_pole_residue_BV_lemma.md), and bounded homogeneous transport on compact open edge segments. A tail-energy absorption would close convergence if far-band dominance were uniform for the entire future orbit.

## Precise unresolved point

The entropy dominance is NOT uniform near the endpoint where another core weight vanishes: a logarithmic term can diverge and permit the near external band to revive. Also off-face transport has a possible boundary-layer g/(u-endpoint) term. Therefore the current work does NOT yet exclude infinitely many edge excursions with deep vertex visits. One needs a local no-return/energy argument across such visits, or a globally uniform corrected transport/entropy invariant. No assertion of a complete s=3 proof is made here.

The case |Splus|=5 can be regarded as an edge with the sixth positive-level root a permanently zero virtual coordinate when that root is distinct. If Q-H has a double positive-level root, one must explicitly use the confluent-factor version (or remain in the five-node a/t formulation); this should not be silently omitted.
''')
print('Wrote global s3 case reduction')

import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
x=P([0,1]);q=(x*x-1)*(x-2)*(x+3);r=(x*x-1)*(x+2)*(x-3);H=1;Q=q*r;poly=Q-H;l=np.sort(poly.roots());betas=np.sort([z.real for z in (Q+H).roots() if abs(z.imag)<1e-8]);ix=[0,1,3,5,7];lv=l[ix];f=np.vander(lv,4,increasing=True);A=np.vstack([np.ones(5),(q(lv)[:,None]*f).T]);w=np.linalg.solve(A,np.r_[1.,np.zeros(4)]);wp=w*q(lv)**2/H;G=f.T@(w[:,None]*f);Gp=f.T@(wp[:,None]*f);Pv=P.fromroots(lv)
for beta in betas:
 fb=np.array([beta**j for j in range(4)]);qb=q(beta);rb=r(beta)
 for gamma in betas:
  if beta==gamma:continue
  fg=np.array([gamma**j for j in range(4)]);qg=q(gamma);rg=r(gamma)
  cc=4*qg*np.linalg.solve(G,fg);ee=-np.linalg.solve(Gp,G@cc);dh=w@(r(lv)*(f@cc));aval=(rb*(fb@cc)+qb*(fb@ee)+dh)/H
  formula=-4*Pv(beta)/(H*qb)*(qb*qb-qg*qg)/(gamma-beta)
  print(beta,gamma,aval,formula,abs(aval-formula));assert abs(aval-formula)<1e-4*max(1,abs(aval))

For a positive integer \(s\), consider the following iteration in exact real arithmetic. Let \(n\ge1\), let \(A\in\mathbb R^{n\times n}\) satisfy \(A^\top=A\) and \(v^\top Av>0\) for every nonzero \(v\in\mathbb R^n\), and choose \(b,x_0\in\mathbb R^n\). Define
\[
x_*=A^{-1}b,\qquad r_k=b-Ax_k,\qquad \|v\|_A=(v^\top Av)^{1/2}.
\]
If \(r_k=0\), terminate. Otherwise, set
\[
\mathcal K_s(A,r_k)=\operatorname{span}\{r_k,Ar_k,\ldots,A^{s-1}r_k\}
\]
and let \(x_{k+1}\) be the unique minimizer of \(\|x_*-z\|_A\) over \(z\in x_k+\mathcal K_s(A,r_k)\). Each such minimization is one restart block, so \(k\) counts blocks. For a nonterminating orbit, define
\[
y_k=\frac{r_k}{\|r_k\|_2},\qquad \|v\|_2=(v^\top v)^{1/2}.
\]
Determine the smallest positive integer \(s\) for which there exist \(n,A,b,x_0\) as above such that \(r_k\ne0\) for every \(k\ge0\), but \((y_{2k})_{k\ge0}\) does not converge in Euclidean norm. Return \(+\infty\) if no such integer exists.

The answer is 4. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at any other answer is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving 4 takes two halves: for every \(s\in\{1,2,3\}\) and every \(n,A,b,x_0\) as above, every nonterminating orbit has \((y_{2k})_{k\ge0}\) converging in Euclidean norm, and for \(s=4\) there exist \(n,A,b,x_0\) as above such that \(r_k\ne0\) for every \(k\ge0\) but \((y_{2k})_{k\ge0}\) does not converge in Euclidean norm. import numpy as np
from numpy.polynomial import Polynomial as P
x=P([0,1]);q=(x*x-1)*(x-2)*(x+3);r=(x*x-1)*(x+2)*(x-3);H=1;Q=q*r;poly=Q-H;l=np.sort(poly.roots());betas=np.sort([z.real for z in (Q+H).roots() if abs(z.imag)<1e-8]);ix=[0,1,3,5,7];lv=l[ix];f=np.vander(lv,4,increasing=True);A=np.vstack([np.ones(5),(q(lv)[:,None]*f).T]);w=np.linalg.solve(A,np.r_[1.,np.zeros(4)]);wp=w*q(lv)**2/H;G=f.T@(w[:,None]*f);Gp=f.T@(wp[:,None]*f);Pv=P.fromroots(lv)
for beta in betas:
 fb=np.array([beta**j for j in range(4)]);qb=q(beta);rb=r(beta)
 for gamma in betas:
  if beta==gamma:continue
  fg=np.array([gamma**j for j in range(4)]);qg=q(gamma);rg=r(gamma)
  cc=4*qg*np.linalg.solve(G,fg);ee=-np.linalg.solve(Gp,G@cc);dh=w@(r(lv)*(f@cc));aval=(rb*(fb@cc)+qb*(fb@ee)+dh)/H
  formula=-4*Pv(beta)/(H*qb)*(qb*qb-qg*qg)/(gamma-beta)
  print(beta,gamma,aval,formula,abs(aval-formula));assert abs(aval-formula)<1e-4*max(1,abs(aval))
